Pattern 1: Template Contamination	Cover sheet has Mountain View references in fire sprinkler note, public works occupancy release, applicable codes footer. SAME issue Bella Casa produced on McFarland AG 0.1. Designer process problem — pre-resubmittal scrub needed on every sheet.
Pattern 2: Code Cycle Drift	Cover sheet uses 2022 codes. Saratoga is now on 2025 cycle for new applications. Vesting policy must be confirmed. McFarland faced same pivot.
Pattern 3: AHJ Note Mix-up	"All fire sprinkler plans shall be submitted directly to the City of Mountain View Fire Department." Should be Santa Clara County Fire Department. Direct reuse of Mountain View boilerplate.
Pattern 4: Razor-Thin Compliance Margins	FAR has 33 SF buffer. Height is at the 26' max. Right side 2nd-floor setback at 13'-4" against 13.2' interpretation. Three independent compliance items have <1% margin. High risk that any correction pushes one over.
Pattern 5: Cross-Sheet Area Inconsistency	Cover sheet shows multiple area tables: Lot Coverage Analysis (5,438 SF), Pervious/Impervious (29,327 SF site total), Net FAR (5,280 SF). Numbers must reconcile across all sheets. Verify cover matches AA 2.6 Plot Plan and AA 2.7 Area Schedule.
Pattern 6: Cross-Set Coordination Failure	SARATOGA CREEK on the property is shown on NNR topographic survey but completely absent from architectural cover sheet, civil cover sheet, civil utility plan, and elsewhere. Multiple project-defining facts (creek presence, heritage trees, EXCEPTION area on survey) live only in the survey document and have not propagated to other sheets. Bella Casa generates architectural sheets without reading the survey. Civil sheets reference wrong geotech firm (Frank Lee Fremont). FF elevations differ by 0.37 ft between civil and architectural. Sheet count differs between civil index and architectural cover. Cross-set coordination is broken at every interface. Same systemic issue as McFarland but worse here because of the creek.
Pattern 7: Consultants Working in Parallel Universes	Aesculus arborist (Nov 2025) understood project scope as house remodel + ADU. Bella Casa cover sheet (March 2026) describes scope as full demo + new house + ADU. NNR survey was reviewed by arborist; Bella Casa was apparently never given the arborist report; civil set has no tree references; nobody has the Saratoga Creek constraint on their sheets. Three independent consultants did high-quality work on three different versions of the project. Until project scope is locked across all consultants, every resubmittal will compound errors. The McFarland project showed similar coordination drift but Burns Way is significantly worse because of (a) the creek constraint, (b) 30 protected trees, and (c) razor-thin FAR margin that does not survive scope changes.
Pattern 8: MEP-Architectural Coordination Drift	MEP set has same pathologies as architectural set. Equipment list says gas water heater while ELEC plans show 220V outlet — designer producing two stories at once. Heat-pump system but gas-furnace combustion-air notes carried over. Attic FURNACE notes labelled while equipment is air handler. MERV 6 filter spec outdated. ASHRAE 62.2 missing. Whole-house ventilation strategy undefined. HV/PL 1.2 sheet titled First Floor but plan is Second Floor. CWS vs SJW utility name confusion. ELEC keynote 14 says 50VA where it should be 50A 240V. Legend typos MONOOXIDE and REFRIDGERATOR. Pattern 1 (template contamination) + Pattern 6 (deferred professional judgment) + Pattern 7 (consultant drift) all present in MEP set. Same Bella Casa output discipline issues.

####################################################################################################
SHEET: 2. Review Protocol
####################################################################################################

REVIEW PROTOCOL — SEVERITY, CATEGORIES, STATUS, SOURCE

Topic / Term	Definition / Scope
APPROACH	Sheet-by-sheet review for internal QA. Source-by-source for AHJ findings (Saratoga Planning, SCCFD, 4LEAF). Each finding numbered uniquely.
FINDING ID FORMAT	AHJ findings: SAR-F### (Saratoga), FIRE-F### (SCCFD), CIV-### (4LEAF civil — preserves their original numbering like CIV-E1, CIV-G1). Internal QA: INT-F### per project, or [SheetCode]-F### per drawing sheet (e.g., AG01-F001).
SOURCE FIELD	Every finding has a Source: AHJ-Saratoga Planning | AHJ-SCCFD Fire | AHJ-4LEAF Civil/PW | Internal QA. This drives ownership and response strategy.
ROUND	Tracks city correction round. Round 1 is current. Resubmittal will trigger Round 2 comments. Future rounds tracked in same finding row by adding Round 2 Status, etc.
CODE COLUMNS	Each finding checked against 2022 code (cover sheet basis) AND 2025 code (current cycle). Vesting determination drives which is binding.
SEVERITY	DEFINITION
Critical	Will block resubmittal acceptance OR cause life-safety failure. Must be fully resolved before resubmittal package goes back to Saratoga.
Major	AHJ correction-letter item that will hold up Design Review approval or cause Round 2 rejection. Resolve before resubmittal.
Minor	Plan check may or may not enforce. Good practice to resolve. Document either way.
Observation	Not a defect. Internal note for client awareness or design quality.
STATUS	MEANING
Open	Raised, not yet addressed by team.
In Progress	Assigned to team member or consultant; work underway.
Resolved	Fix made and verified against latest sheet revision.
Awaiting Consultant	Sent to civil / structural / geotech / Title 24 / arborist; waiting on their deliverable.
Awaiting AHJ Clarification	Need a clarifying email or call from Saratoga / SCCFD / 4LEAF to confirm correct response approach.
Awaiting Client	Client decision required (e.g., deed restriction signing).
Deferred	Parked intentionally; will be addressed as deferred submittal (e.g., fire sprinkler plans, EWAS).
Not Applicable	Reviewed and determined not to apply.
CATEGORY	SCOPE
Jurisdictional	Wrong AHJ referenced (e.g., Mountain View text on Saratoga project — see Pattern 1).
Zoning	Saratoga Muni Code Art 15 — setbacks, FAR, coverage, height, hardscape limits.
Calculation	Area tabulations, FAR math, slope deduction, setback math, cross-table consistency.
Code-CBC	California Building Code.
Code-CRC	California Residential Code — egress, smoke/CO, R313 sprinklers, R337 WUI.
Code-T24	Title 24 Part 6 energy.
Code-CALGreen	Title 24 Part 11.
Code-CFC	California Fire Code + Saratoga local fire amendments.
Code-CEC	Electrical.
Code-CMC	Mechanical.
Code-CPC	Plumbing.
Drafting	Line work, template artifacts, legends, schedules.
Notes	General notes, sheet notes, code citations.
Drawing List	Sheet index errors.
Cross-Sheet Coord	Same item shown differently on multiple sheets.
Professional	License numbers, seals, stamps, responsible party.
Stormwater/NPDES	NPDES, C.3, SCVURPPP, LID, infiltration.
Civil/Grading	Grading, drainage, cut/fill, slope grading, sewer/water utilities.
Fire Access	SCCFD A-1 roadways, D-1 turnarounds, hose pull, hydrant flow, fire lane signs.
Tree Protection	Saratoga Heritage Tree ord, arborist requirements, dripline encroachment.
WUI	Wildland-Urban Interface — CBC Ch 7A, CRC R337, materials.
Vaastu	Firm standard — orientation and zonal compliance.
Miscellaneous	Anything else.

Min Lot Area	15,000 SF	Art 15-12.050	Lot is 29,305 SF — well over minimum
Site Slope (this lot)	12.5% (avg, surveyor-confirmed)	SMC 15-06.630	Triggers FAR slope deduction (16% area off lot for FAR calc)
Net Site Area (post-slope)	24,616 SF (29,305 × 0.84)	SMC 15-06.630	FAR computed from net, not gross
Front Setback	25'-0" min (interior lot)	Art 15-12.090(a)	Cover sheet: 1st flr 44'-7" / 2nd flr 66'-6" — complies

FAR Calculation Steps (Burns Way)	1. Slope deduction: 29,305 × 0.16 = 4,689 SF off / 2. Net: 29,305 - 4,689 = 24,616 SF / 3. Over 15,000: 24,616 - 15,000 = 9,616 → ~10,000 SF / 4. Bonus: 10 × 78 = 780 SF / 5. Subtotal: 4,050 + 780 = 4,830 SF / 6. Restricted-deed adj: 4,830 × 1.10 = 5,313 SF	Art 15-45.030	ADU getting deed restriction enables 10% FAR bonus. 33 SF buffer between 5,280 SF achieved and 5,313 SF max.

Min Off-Street Parking	2 covered + 2 uncovered (R-1)	Art 15-35	MH garage 612 SF (2 cars covered) + driveway uncovered — verify
Heritage Tree Ordinance	Native oaks ≥10" DBH and other protected species	Art 15-50	Aesculus arborist on team. AA 2.1/2.2 tree protection plan required.
Design Review Trigger	2-story OR >6,000 SF gross OR >18' height OR slope >10%	Art 15-45.060	TRIGGERED on multiple counts. ADR26-0004 = Administrative Design Review path.

THE FIVE DECISIONS PRINCIPAL NEEDS THIS WEEK
Decision	Question / Owner	Why This Week
1. ADU Deed Restriction	Question for Owner: Will Sriram accept a 30-year (or whatever Saratoga requires) deed-restriction on the ADU to claim the 10% FAR bonus?	WHY THIS WEEK: If owner declines, FAR cap drops from 5,313 to 4,830 SF. Current 5,280 SF design exceeds. Triggers full redesign. Linked to M-15, SAR-F001, RFI-014.
2. Gas vs All-Electric	Question for Principal + FRI Energy: Lock the equipment decision. Saratoga reach code presumes all-electric for new construction. Gas TWH listed in equipment but ELEC plans show 220V.	WHY THIS WEEK: Cascades to electrical service size (M-02), Title 24 model (M-19), gas service retention or elimination, FAR (gas equipment takes exterior space), and MEP cleanup (delete or keep ~8 different gas-related notes). Linked to M-01.
3. Code Cycle 2022 vs 2025	Question for Saratoga (RFI-001): What is the binding code cycle for ADR26-0004? Vesting only if filed before 1-Jan-2026. Drives every consultant deliverable.	WHY THIS WEEK: T24 model rerun (FRI Energy), all code citations on cover and consultant sheets, WUI material specs, MEP filter spec MERV 6 vs 13, ventilation rule R303 vs ASHRAE 62.2. Linked to M-11.
4. Height Methodology	Question for Saratoga (RFI-003): Confirm the 26-ft maximum methodology. Cover sheet shows 518.81 - 492.73 = 26.08 ft, which is over by 0.08 ft.	WHY THIS WEEK: Either Saratoga accepts methodology and design needs a 6-12 inch ridge reduction, or Saratoga sets a different basis. Either path requires 1-2 weeks of redrawing. Linked to M-16, SAR-F003.
5. PG&E Service Application	Decision for Principal: When to file PG&E service-upgrade application? Likely 320A or 400A meter-main needed for all-electric scope.	WHY THIS WEEK: PG&E lead time in Bay Area is 6-12 months. Filing now (even before final load calc) establishes queue position. Linked to M-02, RFI-015.

RISK SUMMARY — WHAT HAPPENS IF THESE DECISIONS SLIP
If decision 1 slips: ADU may not qualify for FAR bonus and current design exceeds cap. Worst case: full redesign 4-8 weeks plus consultant rework.
If decision 2 slips: T24 model cannot be finalized, MEP set stays in coordination conflict, electrical load calc cannot be sized, PG&E ESR cannot be filed. Compounding 1-2 weeks per week of slip.
If decision 3 slips: Saratoga Round 2 will reject for code-cycle inconsistency. T24 model risk of complete rerun. WUI material specs may need re-pricing.
If decision 4 slips: Round 2 rejection likely. 1-2 week redesign once methodology known.
If decision 5 slips: PG&E lead time consumed. Build schedule constrained by service-upgrade availability not by construction. Risk of CofO delay 6+ months.
CUMULATIVE RISK: If 2 or more decisions slip, project loses 2-3 months of schedule and consultant fees compound. 4LEAF civil + Saratoga planning + SCCFD fire all rotate review staff so each delay also re-queues review position.

HANDOFF NOTES TO PRINCIPAL
• The 20 master findings in tab 9 are the most useful artifact for client and consultant communication. Each master clears a cluster of sheet-level findings.
• The prioritized action list in tab 10 is sequenced for actual execution. The This Week section is calibrated to what one principal + designer can move in 5 working days.
• The 18 RFIs in tab 8 should be sent out as a batch, not one at a time. Saratoga, SCCFD, 4LEAF, PG&E, SJW each get their own consolidated RFI document so they can respond once.
• The detailed sheet-by-sheet finding tabs (44 of them) are the workhorse for designer corrections. Bella Casa should be given direct access and asked to mark Round 2 status as they fix.
• Pattern 8 (MEP-architectural coordination drift) is the same pattern McFarland showed but compounded across more disciplines. Recommend adopting a formal pre-resubmittal coordination meeting with Bella Casa, FRI, civil, and structural in one room before any further drawing iterations.
• FAR margin (33 SF), height (26-0 at max), right side setback (13-4 vs 13.2), and front yard hardscape (60.4% over 50% max) are four independent compliance items each at <1% margin. Any one geometry change risks pushing one over. Recommend lock-down of geometry before MEP detailing proceeds.
